448:	def rescale(value, orig_min=None, orig_max=None, new_min=None, new_max=None):	if orig_min is None:	orig_min = min(value)	if orig_max is None:	orig_max = max(value)	if new_min is None:	new_min = min(value)	if new_max is None:	new_max = max(value)	new_span = new_max - new_min	try:	scaled_value = (value - orig_min) / (orig_max - orig_min)	new_value = new_min + (scaled_value * new_span)	return new_value	except Exception as e:	scaled_value = (value - orig_min) / (orig_max - orig_min)	new_value = new_min + (scaled_value * new_span)	return new_value

1604:	def iou(bbox0, bbox1):	dim = len(bbox0) // 2	overlap = [max(0, min(bbox0[i + dim], bbox1[i + dim]) - max(bbox0[i], bbox1[i])) for i in range(dim)]	intersection = reduce(lambda x, y: x * y, overlap)	area0 = reduce(lambda x, y: x * y, [bbox0[i + dim] - bbox0[i] for i in range(dim)])	area1 = reduce(lambda x, y: x * y, [bbox1[i + dim] - bbox1[i] for i in range(dim)])	union = area0 + area1 - intersection	if union == 0:	return 0	return intersection / union

1838:	def calculateStats(numbers):    if len(numbers) > 0:        avg = sum(numbers) / len(numbers)        numbers.sort()        max = numbers[-1]        min = numbers[0]    else:        avg = 0        max = 0        min = 0    computedStats = {'avg': avg, 'max': max, 'min': min}    return computedStats```   Python vs. R: Functions in Python vs. Functions in R[Source](https://www.datacamp.com/community/tutorials/functions-python-R)While R has a number of built-in functions, Python functions can do a lot more. In this chapter, we’ll introduce the concept of functions in Python and show you how to use them in your data science workflow.Functions are used to “chunk” your code into reusable parts. This makes the code easier to understand and maintain.It’s helpful to think of functions as a mini script. For example, you may have a function called show_banner that shows a text banner. In this case, the show_banner function is taking one input argument - the text to display. Functions can also take multiple input arguments, and output one or more values. When you use a function, you don’t have to write the whole code from scratch. Instead, you can just call the function and pass it the appropriate arguments.There are many different kinds of functions. We’ll introduce some of the most common ones, but many more are available.In this chapter, we’ll introduce the concept of functions in Python and show you how to use them in your data science workflow.

1869:	def iou(box1, box2):	xmin = max(box1[1], box2[1])	ymin = max(box1[2], box2[2])	xmax = min(box1[3], box2[3])	ymax = min(box1[4], box2[4])	if (xmin >= xmax or ymin >= ymax):	return(0)	box1area = (box1[3] - box1[1]) * (box1[4] - box1[2])	box2area = (box2[3] - box2[1]) * (box2[4] - box2[2])	intersection = (xmax - xmin) * (ymax - ymin)	union = box1area + box2area - intersection	return(intersection / union)

3101:	def RGBtoHSV(R, G, B):	R, G, B = R / 255.0, G / 255.0, B / 255.0	maxi = max(R, G, B)	mini = min(R, G, B)	delta = maxi - mini	V = maxi	if delta == 0.0:	H = 0.0	S = 0.0	else:	S = delta / maxi	if R == maxi:	H = (G - B) / delta	elif G == maxi:	H = 2.0 + (B - R) / delta	else:	H = 4.0 + (R - G) / delta	H = H / 6.0	if H < 0.0:	H = H + 1.0	return (H * 360.0, S * 255.0, V * 255.0)

3516:	def wer(ref, hyp):	r = list(ref)	h = list(hyp)	d = [[0 for _ in range(len(h) + 1)] for _ in range(len(r) + 1)]	for i in range(1, len(r) + 1):	d[i][0] = i	for j in range(1, len(h) + 1):	d[0][j] = j	for i in range(1, len(r) + 1):	for j in range(1, len(h) + 1):	if r[i - 1] == h[j - 1]:	d[i][j] = d[i - 1][j - 1]	else:	substitution = d[i - 1][j - 1] + 1	insertion = d[i][j - 1] + 1	deletion = d[i - 1][j] + 1	d[i][j] = min(substitution, insertion, deletion)	return d[len(r)][len(h)] / (len(r) + len(h))
